# Ayush In India 2024

## Page 1

1 
 
INTRODUCTION 
 
Government of India created a full-fledged Ministry of Ayush on 9 th November, 2014 
by upgrading the Department of Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy (ISM&H) 
with a mandate to promote and propagate Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy (see 
Box1 for Allocation of Business to the Ministry). The Ministry is committed to infuse the 
wisdom of traditional medicine with the methodologies of modern science; scientifically 
validating the systems and presenting them in the scientific idiom, relating their efficacy to 
modern life styles (see the Vision and Mission on the inside the back cover). 
 Box 1: Business Allocation of the Ministry of Ayush* 
1. Formulation of policy and policy issues for development and propagation of 
Traditional & Non -Conventional Systems of Health Care and Healing which 
include Ayurveda, Yoga, Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, Sowa Rigpa and 
Homoeopathy etc. (Ayush systems of Health Care). 
2. Development and implementation of programmes including Central schemes and 
Centrally sponsored schemes for development and propagation of Ayush systems 
of Health Care. 
3. Co-ordination and promotion of research and development including assistance 
therefor in Ayush systems of Health Care. 
4. Setting up and maintenance of c entral institutions for research and development, 
education and standards relating to Ayush systems of Health Care. 
5. All issues and matters requiring action at the level of Government in regard to- 
(a) Pharmacopoeia Commission for Indian Medicine and Homoeopathy; 
(b) Omitted; 
(c) Central Council of Indian Medicines; 
(d) Central Council of Homoeopathy; 
(e) Ayurvedic Pharmacopoeia Committee; 
(f) Homoeopathic Pharmacopoeia Committee; 
(g) Unani Pharmacopoeia Committee; 
(h) Siddha Pharmacopoeia Committee; 
(i) Ayurvedic, Siddha and Unani Drugs Technical Advisory Board; 
(j) Central Research Councils and National Institutes relating to Indian Systems of 
Medicine and Homoeopathy. 
6. Education, Training and Research in all aspects of Indian Systems of Medicine 
including higher training abroad. 
7. Matters of cadre formation and control including framing and amendment of 
recruitment rules, recruitment, promotion and all other service matters relating to 
Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy doctors of Central Government 
Health Scheme and other Central Government institutions requiring action at 
Government level. Note. - Day-to-day administration and management will 
continue to be with the Director, Central Government Health Scheme. 
8. Liaison with foreign countries and international bodies as regards matters relating 
to Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy.

A. Ayush Systems 
Ayush include Ayurveda, Yoga & Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, Sowa Rigpa and 
Homoeopathy. Ayurveda is the oldest system with a documented history of its practice for 
more than 5000 years whereas Homoeopathy is i n practice in India for around 2 00 years. 
These systems are being practised in the country with diverse preferences of people and 
infrastructural facilities. Ayush systems of medicine include Indian Systems of Medicine and 
Homoeopathy. 
 The Indian System of Medicine is of great lineage. It is the culmination of Indian 
thought of medicine which represents a way of healthy living valued with a long and unique 
cultural history and epitomizes the v edic guidance of ‘Let noble thoughts come to us from 
every side’. Thus, one can see an amalgamation of the best of influences that came in from 
the contact of different sources of knowledge. While Ayurveda, Siddha and Y oga has been a 
part of ancient v edic tradition our scriptures and associ ated with sages like Charaka, 
Sushrutha, Agasthya and Patanjali, assimilating practices of other civilizations enriched this 
tradition. Unani Medicine has roots in Greece, H omoeopathy in Germany, and Naturopathy 
in the 19th century Natural Cure Movement of Europe. In parallel to the multifaceted cultural 
evolution of our country, Ayush medicinal systems have evolved over centuries blessed with 
a plethora of traditional medicinal practices.

## Page 3

3 

(i) Ayurveda: 
 Ayurveda is the time tested traditional system of medicine of India that explains the 
cause of different miseries, imparts the knowledge of life and advocates promotion of 
physical, mental and spiritual wellbeing. It is believed that Ayurveda is originated out of 
Vedas (particularly Rigveda and Atharvaveda). Numerous references of health, diseases, their 
treatment as well as use of non -materialistic things such as sunrays, fasting, mantra etc; are 
available in these Vedas. The knowledge of Ayurveda was first comprehensively documented 
in the compendia like ‘ Brahma Samhita ’, ‘Ágniveshatantra’, ‘ SusrutSamhita’, ‘ Bhela 
Samhita’ etc. 
 According to Ayurveda, health is considered as a basic pre -requisite for achieving the 
goals of life - Dharma ( duties), Arth (finance), Kama (materialistic desires) 
and Moksha (salvation). Ayurveda describes that Satva (mind), Atma (real self) Shareer (the 
body) collectively known as Tri-danda, is essential for the existence of the man. This 
conscious combination supports the man like a tri -pod supports the table, and is the one for 
which Ayurveda is flourished across the ages. Ayurveda states that human is formed up of 
five basic elements i.e. Pancha Mahabhootas, namely: Prithvi (earth), Jal (water), 
Agni (fire), Vayu (air) and Akash (ether) and real self. These five elements are the basic 
building blocks of Tridosha - three groups of factors which participate in the health and 
disease known as the Vata (ether + air), Pitta (fire) and Kapha (earth + water), Saptadhatu- 
seven tissue systems explained in Ayurveda, and Mala- metabolic end products. Furthermore, 
it also explains the individuals’ integral relationship with the nature and opines that human 
body is affected by the daily cosmic changes, seasons and environment and therefore 
describes daily regimen, seasonal regimen and dietary concepts for a healthy life. This 
indicates the system approach of Ayurveda i.e. the interaction of different systems within the 
body and interaction in between body and environment. Ayurveda, being an applied science 
and considering the limitations for printing in ancient time, all the ancient texts have 
concentrated mainly on applied aspects. Understanding of ‘Functional Anatomy’ 
i.e. Shareer is the unique contribution of Ayurveda to the modern science which has great 
potential for new discoveries in system Biology. 
(ii) Unani: 
Unani Medicine (Tibb .) is a medical system with comprehensive solution for the 
various states of human body providing preventive, promotive, curative and rehabilitative 
health care. This system has evolved in different parts of the world by imbibing observations 
and experiments of many civilizations. 
The fundamentals, diagnosis and treatment modalities of the system are based on 
scientific principles. The basic framework of this system is based on the Hippocratic theory 
of four Humours, according to which any disturbance in the equilibrium of humours causes 
disease and therefore the treatment aims at restoring the humoral equilibrium. The system 
also believes that Medicatrix Naturae ( Tabiat Mudabbira-i-Badan) is the supreme power,

## Page 4

4 
 
which controls all the physiological functions of the body, provides resistance against 
diseases and helps in healing naturally. 
The most important principle of Unani Medicine is temperament (Mizäj) which 
classifies human beings, diets, drugs, etc. into four qualitative types: hot and dry, hot and wet, 
cold and wet & cold and dry. The pathological shift in the temperament causes disease, which 
is cured by reversing the abnormal temperament to the normal using drugs and diets of 
opposite temperament. The four humours (Akhlät), i.e. blood (Dam), phlegm (Balgham), 
yellow bile (Safrä’) and black bile (Sawdä’) are the liquid fundamental substances of living 
things possessing ability to support the biological processes, i.e. life, nutrition, growth, 
sensation, movement and reproduction. Human temperament is commonly denoted by the 
dominant humour i.e. sanguineous (Damwi), phlegmatic (Balghami), choleric (Safräwi) and 
melancholic (Sawdäwi), which can be correlated with the temperaments of diets, drugs, 
environmental factors, etc. 
The Unani system of medicine includes the science of maintaining health (Hifzän -I 
Sihhat) which lay importance to the understanding and maintenance of health in a positive 
and individualized manner with the help of different guidelines. Some of the commonly used 
guidelines are summarized as: Regimens for Seasons (Tadäbir -i Mavasim), Regimens for 
Age-groups (Tadäbir -i Asnän), R egimens for Pregnancy (Tadäbir -i Hamal), Regimens for 
Travellers (Tadäbir-i Musäfir) etc. 
(iii) Siddha: 
Siddha is one of the ancient comprehensive medical systems in India. The healing 
science encompasses the physical, mental, social, and spiritual wellbeing by adapting simple 
lifestyle practices, dietary regimens, safe and effective drugs of origin from plants, mineral 
and animal. Siddha medicine focuses on preventive, promotive and rejuvenate methods for 
maintaining good health, defying ageing and curative procedures including cleansing 
therapies and external therapies. The strength of the Siddha medical system lies in its holistic 
approach for a healthy living. 
The Siddha system is evolved based on ninety -six tools or principles (Thathuvas) 
which include physical, physiological, psychological, and intellectual aspects of every human 
being. The main principle in treating diseases through Siddha system is by normalizing the 
altered vital life factors ( Vali, Azhal, Aiyam ). Kayakarpam called rejuvenation elixir in 
Siddha medicine helps in the prevention, treatment of ailments and to maintain healthy long 
life preserving memory, intelligence, healthy ageing. The Siddha diagnostic methodology is 
very peculiar as they assist in diagnosis and prognosis of diseases through Wrist 
Circumferential Sign (Manikkadai Nool ) and eight vital tests ( Ennvagai Thervu ) such as 
pulse reading, examination of the tongue, complexion of body, study of voice, examination of 
eyes, urine examination, ( Neer Kuri & Neikrug ), examination of faeces, status of the 
sensation of human body. 
The main sources of preparation in Siddha Medicine are Plant origin ( Mooligai 
vaguppu), Inorganic/Organic substances (Thathu vaguppu) - metals and minerals and Animal

## Page 5

5 
 
products ( Jeeva vaguppu ).Siddha system classified medications into 64 pharmaceutical 
dosage forms of which 32 are internal medications and 32 are external medication and 
treatment procedures.Siddha medicines with long shelf life such as Parpam , Chenduram, 
Chunnam form with higher efficacy, smaller dosage, easy palatability, long shelf life, easy 
absorption and sustainable quicker results as the nano particles aid in better bio - availability 
and bio-equivalence. Distinctive external treatment procedures such as Pressure Manipulation 
Therapy ( Varmam), Physical Manipulation Therapy ( Thokkanam), Bone setting ( Otivu 
Murivu Maruthuvam) for neuromuscular degeneration disorders and pain management 
(iv) Yoga: 
The word "Yoga" comes from the Sanskrit word "yuj" which means "to unite or 
integrate. "Yoga is about the union of a person's own consciousness and the universal 
consciousness. It is primarily a way of life, first propounded by Maharshi Patanjali in 
systematic form Yogsutra. The discipline of Yoga consists of eight components namely, 
restraint(Yama), observance of austerity(Niyama), physical postures(Asana), breathing 
control(Pranayam), restraining of sense organs (Pratyahar), contemplation(Dharna), 
meditation(Dhyan) and Deep meditation (Samadhi). These steps in the practice of Yoga 
have the potential to elevate social and personal behavior and to promote physical health by 
better circulation of oxygenated blood in the body, restraining the sense organs and there by 
inducing tranquility and serenity of mind and spirit. The practice of Yoga has also been 
found to be useful in the prevention of certain psychosomatic diseases and improves 
individual resistance and ability to endure stressful situations. Yoga is a promotive, 
preventive rehabilitative and curative intervention for overall enhancement of health status. 
A number of postures are described in Yoga literature to improve health, to prevent diseases 
and to cure illness. The physical postures are required to be chosen judiciously and have to 
be practiced in the correct way so that the benefits of prevention of disease, promotion of 
health and therapeutic use can be derived from them. 
 
The United Nations Gener al Assembly had declared June 21 stas the International 
Yoga Day on 11th December, 2014. Every year the Prime Minister of India, leads the nation 
in celebration of IDY. 
 
(v) Naturopathy: 
 Naturopathy is rooted in the healing wisdom of many cultures and times based on the 
principle of natural healing. The principles and practices of Naturopathy are integrated in the 
life style of the people with emphasis on living close to nature. 
 Naturopathy is a cost effective non pharmacological therapies involving the use of 
natural materials for health care and healthy living. It is based on the theories of vitality, 
boosting the self -healing capacity of the body and the principles of healthy living. 
Naturopathy is a system of natural treatment and also a way of life widely practiced, globally 
accepted and recognized for health preservation and management of illnesses. Naturopathy 
advocates living in harmony w ith constructive principles of n ature on the physical, mental,

## Page 6

6 
 
social and spiritual planes. It has great promotive, preventive, curative as well as restorative 
potentials. 
 Naturopathy promotes healing by stimulating the body’s inherent power to regain 
health with the help of five elements of nature – Earth, Water, Air, Fire and Ether. It is a call 
to “Return to Nature” and to resort to a simple way of living in harmony with the self, society 
and environment. Naturopathy advocates ‘Better Health with usage of Natural therapeutics, 
fasting, diet, yoga and physical culture’. It is reported to be effective in chronic, allergic, 
autoimmune, degenerative and stress related disorders. The theory and practice of 
Naturopathy are based on a holistic view point with particular attention to simple eating and 
living habits, adoption of purificatory measures, use of hydrotherapy, cold packs, mud packs, 
baths, massages, fasting etc. 
(vi) Homoeopathy: 
 "Homoeopathy" was introduced as a scientific system of drug therapeutics by a 
German Physician, Dr. Christian Frederick Samuel Hahnemann in 1805. While translating a 
medical treatise by Scottish physician and chemist, William Cullen , from English to 
German, in 1790, he came across a foot note under Cinchona that attributed its fever curing 
property to the astringent (decongestant) qualities of the drug. Being sceptical of Cullen's 
remarks concerning the effect of Cinchona for curing malaria, Hahnemann experimented its 
effect on himself by taking repeated doses of cinchona tincture and experienced fever, 
shivering and joint pains: symptoms similar to those of malarial fever. After series of 
experiments, Hahnemann concluded that a drug that could produce certain symptoms in 
healthy individuals could also cure similar disease symptoms, in accordance with some 
hidden, natural laws of s imilars as had been vaguely perceived by ancient physicians. This 
led to the coining of the word "homoeo-pathy" (which comes from 
the Greek: ὅμοιος hómoios, " -like" and πάθος páthos, "suffering"). Based on this, 
Hahnemann postulated the key principle of Homoeopathy, the Law of Similars , logically 
evolving it as an experimental science, according to the method of inductive reasoning after 
exact observation, correct interpretation, rational explanation and scientific construction. 
(vii) Sowa-Rig-pa (Amchi Medicine) 
Sowa Rigpa “Science of Healing,” is one of the oldest traditional medical systems in 
the world, with roots tracing back thousands of years. The term Sowa Rigpa is derived from 
the Bhoti or Tibetan language, where “Sowa” means “healing” and “Rigpa” means 
“knowledge” or “science.” Together, the term signifies “the knowledge of healing” 
Practitioners of this ancient medical tradition are known as Amchi or Menpa. 
The fundamental text book of Sowa-Rigpa is the Four Tantras (rGyud-bZhi in Tibetan 
or Chatush Tantra in Sanskrit). According to Sowa -Rigpa, rGyud-bZhi teachings were 
originally imparted by Bhagwan Buddha near Bodh Gaya in the sacred forest of Sudarshan 
Van, later on it was propagated by his disciples. Over the centuries, Sowa Rigpa has 
flourished across the Himalayan belt, including regions such as Ladakh, Sikkim, Arunachal 
Pradesh, Himachal Pradesh, and parts of Nepal, Bhutan, and Tibet.

## Page 7

7 

The theoretical framework of Sowa Rigpa is deeply rooted in Buddhist philosophy, 
emphasizing principles such as interdependence, karma, love, and compassion. It is built 
upon several core concepts: 
 • The Five Elements (Byung-ba lnga): Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Space, which 
form the basis of all matter and living beings. 
 • The Three Humours (Nyes -pa gsum): Wind (rLung), Bile (mKhris -pa), and 
Phlegm (Bad-kan), which govern physiological and psychological functions. 
 • The Seven Bodily Constituents (Lus -zungs bdun): Essential bodily tissues 
including nutritional essence, blood, muscle, fat, bone, marrow, and reproductive fluids. 
 • The Three Excretions (Dri -ma gsum): Urine, feces, and sweat, which help 
eliminate waste and toxins from the body. 
 
Sowa Rigpa holds that the root cause of all human suffering and disease lies in 
ignorance (ma-rig-pa) and the three afflictive emotions —desire, hatred, and delusion. These 
inner imbalances disrupt the harmony of the three humours, leading to the manifestation of 
various physical and mental ailments. 
 
Preventive healthcare plays a vital role in Sowa Rigpa. The system places great 
emphasis on proper diet, lifestyle, seasonal routines, and mental discipline as key to 
maintaining health and preventing disorders. Each individual’s constitution is taken into 
consideration, with personalized recommendations provided to harmonize the elements and 
humours within the body. 
Today, Sowa Rigpa continues to be practiced and taught in traditional institutes and is 
recognized as a formal system of medicine in several countries. Its holistic approach, 
integrating body, mind, and environment, makes it not only a healing science but also a path 
to spiritual and physical well-being. 
 
B. SCHEMES OF MINISTRY OF AYUSH 
 
 The Ministry has in place a broad institutional framework to carry out its activities. 
The Ministry administers one Centrally Sponsored Scheme namely National Ayush Mission 
(NAM) and following Central Sector (CS) Schemes viz. 
1. Promotion of Information, Education and Communication in Ayush. 
2. AYURSWASTHYA Yojana. 
3. AYURGYAN 
4. Promotion of International Cooperation. 
5. Ayush Oushadhi Gunvatta evum Uttapadan Samvardhan Yojana (AOGUSY) 
6. Conservation Development and Sustainable Management of Medicinal Plants

## Page 8

8 

C. RESEARCH COUNCILS 
At present, the Ministry has five exclusive Research Councils catering to the research 
in different disciplines of Indian Medicine. The councils that are responsible for the officially 
sponsored research activities are CCRAS for Ayurveda, CCRYN for Yoga and Naturopathy, 
CCRUM for Unani, CCRS for Siddha and CCRH for Homoeopathy. 
i. Central Council for Research in Ayurveda Sciences (CCRAS) 
 The Central Council for Research in Ayurvedic sciences (CCRAS), an autonomous 
body under Ministry of Ayush, Govt. of India is apex body in the country for undertaking, 
coordinating, formulating, developing and promoting research on scientific lines in 
Ayurvedic sciences. The activities are carried out through its 30 Institutes/Centres/Units 
located all over India and through collaborative studies with various Universities, Hospitals 
and Institutes. The research activities of the Council include Medicinal Plant Research, Drug 
Standardization, Pharmacological Research, Clinical Research, Literary Research & 
Documentation, Fundamental Research and Pharmaceutical Research. The Research Oriented 
Public Health Care Programmes (Outreach Activities) i.e. Tribal Health Care Research 
Programme, Ayurveda Mobile Health Care Research Programme and Women & Child 
Health Care Programme under Scheduled Castes Sub Plan (SCSP) and Ayurvedic Health 
Centres under North East Plan are also carried out. The Council also provides Health care 
services through Out –Patient Department (OPD) and In -Patient Departments (IPDs) and 
Special clinics for Geriatric Health care. 
The Council has launched Studentship Program for Ayurveda Research Ken 
(SPARK); Post -Doctoral Fellowship Scheme (PDF); Scope for Mainstreaming Ayurveda 
Research in Teaching Professional (SMART) Programme; Scheme for training in Ayurveda 
Research for PG Scholars (PG-STAR); projects on “Anaemia control among adolescent girls 
through Ayurveda interventions” in the five districts under Mission Utkarsh; “Joint R&D 
and Public Health initiatives of Ministry of Ayush and Ministry of Tribal Affairs to cater the 
health needs of children of Ekalavya Model Residential Schools in different tribal areas of 
the country”, Ayurveda Biology Integrated Health Research Under Ayurgyan Scheme, and 
CCRAS Ayurveda Gyan Naipunya Initiative (CCRAS-AGNI). 
 
ii. Central Council for Research in Siddha (CCRS): 
The Central Council for Research in Siddha (CCRS), an autonomous body under 
Ministry of Ayush, Government of India is an apex body for undertaking, coordinating, 
formulating, developing and promoting research on scientific lines in Siddha. It provides 
health care facilities for various diseases through its nine Peripheral Institutes / Units of 
CCRS. CCRS, a SIRO recognized organization has 9 peripheral Institutes/Units across the 
Country with three in Tamil Nad u, one each in Puducherry, Kerala, Karnataka, New Delhi, 
Andhra Pradesh, and Goa. The research Programme of Central Council for Research in 
Siddha (CCRS) mainly focuses on clinical research including safety and efficacy studies of

## Page 9

9 
 
Siddha drugs, validation of the fundamental principles, drug standardization and quality 
control, survey and cultivation of medicinal plants and literary research. 
 
Research Mandate of CCRS are as follows: 
❖ Validation of classical Siddha formulations and therapies. 
❖ Development of safe, effective Siddha medicines and therapeutic techniques for 
diseases of public health significance. 
❖ Capacity building programmes on Research methodology and drug development for 
academic, research institutes and industry. 
❖ Dissemination of research outcome through publications and research journals. 
❖ Patents and Commercialization of products. 
 
iii. Central Council for Research in Unani Medicine (CCRUM): 
 
The Central Council for Research in Unani Medicine (CCRUM) is an autonomous 
organization under the Ministry of Ayush, Government of India. The Council was 
established on 30 thMarch 1978 under Societies Registration Act, XXI of 1860. However, it 
started functioning independently from 10 th January 1979. The council’s research 
programmes comprise clinical research, drug standardization research, literary research and 
survey and cultivation of medicinal plants. Besides, research -oriented extension health 
services and Information, Education & Communication (IEC) activities are also part of the 
Council’s programme. The Council has continued M.D. and Ph.D. programmes in Unani 
Medicine which was started in the previous year. Research activities are being carried out 
through a network of 23 institutes / units functioning in different parts of the country. 

iv. Central Council for Research in Yoga & Naturopathy (CCRYN): 
 
Central Council for Research in Yoga & Naturopathy (CCRYN) is a 
registered autonomous institution engaged in Research, Promotion, Propagation and 
Development in Yoga and Naturopathy in the country since 1978. The Council is fully funded by 
Ministry of Ayush, Govt. of India. 
v. Central Council for Research in Homoeopathy (CCRH): 
Central Council for Research in Homoeopathy (CCRH) is an apex organization 
undertaking research in Homoeopathy. It is an autonomous body under the Ministry of 
Ayush, Government of India. CCRH is engaged in multidimensional research in the field of 
survey & collection of medicinal plants, drug standardization, drug proving, clinical 
verification, clinical research, clinical validation and fundamental research. The objectives 
are to validate homoeopathic principles and drugs; develops new strategies and drugs for 
treating prevalent clinical conditions, including infectious and non -communicable diseases. 
Studies are designed and implemented at all levels of health care, including health promotion, 
disease prevention, treatment and rehabilitation. Clinical research studies are conducted on

## Page 10

10 
 
various disease conditions in clinical and epidemic settings. Drug research includes 
physicochemical, pharmacognostic and pharmacological activities of the raw drug substances 
and finished products. Additionally, drug proving studies determine the pathogenetic 
properties of the drugs to develop the homoeopathic “materia medica” of prescribing 
indications of medicines. Clinical verification and validation studies enhance the utility of 
medicines in disease prevention and treatment. it is also actively engaged in collaborating 
with institutes of excellence both in India and abroad for basic and fundamental research. 
D. NATIONAL INSTITUTES 
The ministry is also at the aegis of eleven professional research institutes and 
academic faculties devoted to various forms of alternative medicine . So far, Twelve National 
Institutes for Ayush i.e. (NIA, Jaipur), (RAV, Ne w Delhi), (AIIA, New Delhi) & (ITRA , 
Jamnagar), Siddha (NIS, Chennai), Unani (NIUM, Bangalore), Yoga (MDNIY, New Delhi), 
Naturopathy (NIN, Pune) , Sowa -Rigpa (NISR, Leh) and Homoeopathy (NIH, Kolkata), 
(NEIAH, Meghalaya), (NEIFM, Pasighat) are existing at national level for teaching, research 
and clinical practices. The resou rces of Central Sector Schemes are channelled through these 
autonomous bodies. 
I. National Institute of Ayurveda (NIA), Jaipur: 
The National Institute of Ayurveda, Deemed to be University (De novo) a top -tier 
autonomous institution, operates under the auspices of the Indian government's Ministry of 
Ayush. The Honourable Prime Minister of India, Shri Narendra Modi, on November 13, 
2020, proclaimed NIA to be the first Central Government -funded institution to be recognised 
as a university in the realm of AYUSH. 
NIA is a premier institute under the Ministry in the field of Ayurveda education, 
Training, Research and Patient care. A number of committees exist for Academic, Research, 
Patient care, Administrative related works like Scientific Advisory Committee, Institutional 
Ethics Committee, Research review board etc. 
NIA is a NAAC ‘A’ accredited institute that offers academic programmes like 
Undergraduate (BAMS), Postgraduate (MD/MS in Ayurveda), Postdoctoral (PhD in 
Ayurveda), and Diploma programs. Additionally, NIA offers six interdisciplinary M.Sc. 
courses in the Ayurvedic area and also offers a wide range of Ayurvedic certificate and short -
term courses. 
With 280 beds, NIA's NABH -accredited hospital provides top -notch OPD and IPD 
services to public. The NIA hospital has a state-of-the-art Panchakarma Centre, contemporary 
OTs, ventilator -supported emergency care beds, a GMP -certified pharmacy, and a NABL 
accredited patholab. 
NIA carries out high -quality, multidisciplinary Ayurvedic research that includes 
laboratory-based, in-vitro, in-vivo, clinical, and literary investigations. To assist these studies, 
the Institute boasts top -notch facilities, including an animal house, a pharmacognosy lab, a 
phytochemistry lab, a drug testing lab, and an advanced simulation lab.

## Page 11

11 
 
II. Rashtriya Ayurveda Vidyapeeth (RAV), New Delhi: 
The Rashtriya Ayurveda Vidyapeeth (RAV) is an autonomous organization under the 
Ministry of Ayush, Govt. of India. The Vidyapeeth was established with the main aim to 
preserve and arrange transfer of Ayurvedic knowledge possessed by eminent Ayurvedic 
scholars and practitioners, to the younger generation through the Indian Traditional Guru –
Shishya method of education and knowledge transfer. 
The Vidyapeeth organizes National Seminars to discuss the latest development and 
research in Ayurveda for the benefits of practitioners and researchers and also conducts 
Interactive Workshops for the discussion on debatable topics of Ayurveda between students 
and teachers. Besides that, RAV also conducts training programmes for Ayurvedic teachers 
on Samhita based clinical diagnosis and for PG students on Research Methodology, 
Manuscript writing and Career opportunities. 
III. Institute of Teaching and Research in Ayurveda (ITRA), Jamnagar (GUJARAT): 
Institute of Teaching and Research in Ayurveda (ITRA), Jamnagar is a premier 
Institute of Ayurveda with status of Institute of National importance and is fully financed 
Institute by Ministry of Ayush, Government of India . It was formerly known as Institute for 
Post Graduate Teaching & Research in Ayurveda (IPGT&RA) under the management of 
Gujarat Ayurved University Jamnagar till October 2020 . The Institute is re -designated as 
collaborating center for traditional medicines (Ayurveda) by W.H.O. till 2023 . Institute is 
aimed to meet current requirements by incorporating advances of basic sciences, serve the 
requirement of global health care, for collaborative research to revalidate traditional claims 
and principles, enrich pharmacopoeia through evidence -based research, mainstream 
Ayurveda in the public health and support and enrich National Health Mission 
 
IV. National Institute of Unani Medicine (NIUM), Bengaluru: 
The National Institute of Unani Medicine (NIUM), established in 1984, has 
significantly contributed to the advancement of Unani Medicine globally. NIUM offers 
postgraduate courses in various disciplines with a vision to excel in education, research, and 
patient care in the Unani System of Medicine. 
In collaboration with esteemed scientific institutions, NIUM fosters research 
standards, publishes bi -annual journals in English and Urdu, and organizes academic events 
such as CMEs, workshops, seminars, and guest lectures. 
India's commitment to Unani Medicine is exemplified by NIUM's achievements, 
notably as the first NABH -accredited Unani hospital. NIUM Bangalore's laboratories, 
including the Central Instrumentation Facility Lab and a Herbal Garden, showcase 
innovation. 
The newly inaugurated NIUM Ghaziabad, a satellite institute of NIUM Bangalore, 
stands as a monumental achievement, featuring 200 -bed attached hospital equipped with 
state-of-the-art facilities. 
 
V. National Institute of Siddha (NIS), Chennai:

## Page 12

12 
 
National Institute of Siddha is an autonomous premiere Institution under the Ministry 
of Ayush, Government of India. This institute was inaugurated on 3 rd September, 2005. The 
mandates of the Institute are as under: - 
 
• To impart Under -Graduate, Post-Graduate, and Ph.D. education programs in Siddha 
System of Medicine. 
• To conduct experiments and to develop pattern of teaching in UG & PG education in 
Siddha System. 
• To conduct research on various aspects of Siddha. 
• To act as a centre of excellence in Siddha. 
• To provide medical care through Siddha system of medicine to general public. 
• To develop, promote and propagate the science and art 
of Siddha. 
 
VI. Morarji Desai National Institute of Yoga (MDNIY), New Delhi: 
 
Morarji Desai National Institute of Yoga (MDNIY) is an autonomous organisation 
registered under the Societies Registration Act, 1860 and functioning under Ministry of 
Ayush, Govt. of India. MDNIY came into existence on 01.04.1998 by upgrading erstwhile 
Central Research Institute for Yoga (CRIY), which was established in the year 1976. 
 
Objectives 
▪ To act as a centre of excellence in Yoga; 
▪ To develop, promote and propagate the philosophy, science and art of Yoga; and 
▪ To provide and promote facilities for yoga education, training, therapy and research to 
fulfil the above two objectives. 
 
VII. National Institute of Naturopathy (NIN), Pune: 
 
National Institute of Naturopathy (NIN) is an autonomous body under the Ministry of 
Ayush, Govt. of India, located at a historical place called “Bapu Bhavan”, named after 
Mahatma Gandhi, who made this institution his home. NIN was established in 22 -12-1986 
with the objectives of propagating and promoting Naturopathy and Yoga, to provide 
treatment facilities through Naturopathy and Yoga, to conduct research and training and to 
establish a living Memorial of Mahatma Gandhi. Another main objective of NIN is to create a 
Nature Cure University – which was one of the aims of forming the “All India Nature Cure 
Foundation Trust” by Mahatma Gandhi. 
 
VIII. National Institute of Homoeopathy (NIH), Kolkata: 
 
National Institute of Homoeopathy was established on 10th December 1975 in Kolkata 
as an Autonomous Institute under the Ministry of Health & Family Welfare, presently under 
the Ministry of Ayush, Govt. of India. The Institute was affiliated to the University of 
Calcutta up to session 2003 -04 and from 2004 -05 has been affiliated to the West Bengal

## Page 13

13 
 
University of Health Sciences, Kolkata. The aim of this institute is to foster excellence in 
Homoeopathic Medical Education and Research, to educate and train undergraduate, post 
graduate students and research scholars of homoeopathy in accordance with highest 
professional standards. The 'APEX' body of the Institute is the Governing Body (GB) headed 
by the Hon'ble Union Minister of State for Ayush. The Governing Body appoints an Institute 
Finance Committee (IFC) to oversee the financial affairs of the Institute. There is a 
Secretariat set up in the Ministry of Ayush who monitor and support the entire Administrative 
and Financial affairs of the institute. The Chief Executive Officer of the institute is the 
Director. 
 
IX. All India Institute of Ayurveda (AIIA), New Delhi & Goa: 
All India Institute of Ayurveda (AIIA), is conceived as an apex Ayurveda institute 
under the Ministry of Ayush, Govt of India with a vision to be an outstanding center of 
excellence for Ayurveda Education, Research, and Healthcare. AIIA is a holistic tertiary care 
Ayurveda treatment center and 200 bedded referral hospital supported by the state -of-art 
modern diagnostic tools and techniques. It aims to bring synergy between traditional 
Ayurveda wisdom and modern tools & technology through scientific validation of 
fundamental principles and human resource development programs. In 2017, the institute was 
inaugurated and dedicated to the nation by the Hon’ble PM and has been offering PG & PhD 
programs in 12 Ayurveda specialties. The services of the institute are accredited by NABH, 
ISO 9001:2015, and NAAC (A++) and catering to the need of the society. The institute is 
serving the society with daily avg. 2000-2500 footfall of OPD patients through its specialized 
44 OPDs (e.g. Diabetes, Respiratory, Musculoskeletal, Neurology, Nephrology, Infertility, 
PCOD & Menopause, Ano-rectal Clinic, Yoga, Life style & Diet Clinic etc). 
AIIA Goa was also inaugurated and dedicated to the nation by the Hon’ble PM on 
11th December 2022, with approved 100 batch strength per year or UG and recently PG 
programs in 5 subjects were introduced after approval from National Commission for Indian 
System of Medicine (NCISM)." 
 
X. North Eastern Institute of Ayurveda & Homoeopathy (NEIAH), Shillong, 
Meghalaya: 
 North Eastern Institute of Ayurveda & Homoeopathy (NEIAH), Shillong is an 
autonomous Institute under the Ministry of Ayush, Government of India. The Institute was 
established in the year 2008 to provide under -graduate, post -graduate, doctoral and post -
doctoral teaching, research facilities and quality patient care services under the Ayurvedic 
and Homoeopathic systems of medicine. It was sanctioned with capacity of 100 -bedded 
Ayurveda Hospital and 50-bedded Homoeopathy Hospital. 
 
XI. North Eastern Institute of Ayurveda & Folk Medicine Research (NEIAFMR), 
Pasighat: 
 
 North Eastern Institute of Ayurveda & Folk Medicine Research (NEIAFMR) was 
established in the year 2008. The institute is spread over an area of 40 acres and is located at

## Page 14

14 
 
Pasighat, East Siang District, Arunachal Pradesh. NEI AFMR is the lone institute in the 
country dedicated for R&D activities on Folk medicine. The mandatory objectives of 
establishing the institute are as follows: 
 
▪ To functions as apex research centre for all aspects of folk medicine knowledge with 
linkages/networking with other research centres. 
▪ To create an interface between traditional healers and scientific research. 
▪ Survey, documentation and validation of folk medicine practices, remedies and 
therapies for possible usage in public healthcare and further research. 
▪ Generation of public awareness about the potentials of folk medicine for enhancing 
public health and rural communities in particular. 
▪ To enhance capacities upgrade skills of traditional healers to enable delivery of 
standardized healthcare services, increasing robustness and sustainability of the 
profession. 
 
XII. National Institute of Sowa Rigpa (NISR), Leh 
 
The National Institute of Sowa Rigpa (NISR), located in Leh, is an autonomous 
Institute functioning under the Ministry of A yush, Government of India. Established in 2020, 
the institute is dedicated to the preservation, promotion, and advancement of the Sowa Rigpa 
medical system - one of the oldest traditional systems of medicine practiced in the Himalayan 
region. NISR aims to serve as a model institution by offering high -quality education, 
professional training, and research opportunities in Sowa Rigpa, alongside delivering 
effective patient care services. 
 
In addition to its academic and healthcare roles, the institute is committed to fostering 
a scientific outlook within the Sowa Rigpa tradition, thereby integrating ancient wisdom with 
contemporary scientific methodologies. 
NISR is registered under the Societies Registration Act XXI of 1860 in the Union 
Territory of Ladakh, with its registered office situated in Leh. It is affiliated with the 
University of Ladakh, ensuring academic credibility and adherence to recognized educational 
standards. 
 
E. STATUTORY REGULATORY BODIES 
E.1 National Commission for Indian System of Medicine (NCISM) 
To ensure transparency and to improve the quality and functioning of ASUSR&H 
Colleges across the country, the National Commission for Indian System of Medicine Act, 
2020 was notified and published on 21st September 2020 in the Official Gazette of India. 
NCISM is a statutory body constituted under NCISM Act, 2020. This Act was 
enacted to ensure a medical education system that improves access to quality and affordable 
medical education, ensures availability of adequate and high -quality medical professionals of 
Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy in all parts of the country.

## Page 15

15 
 
This Act helps to promote equitable and universal healthcare that encourages 
community health perspective and makes services of such medical professionals accessible 
and affordable to all the citizens: - 
i. that promotes national health goals; 
ii. that encourages such medical professionals to adopt latest medical research in their 
work and to contribute to research; 
iii. that has an objective periodic and transparent assessment of medical institutions and 
facilitates maintenance of a medical register of Indian System of Medicine for India 
and enforces high ethical standards in all aspects of medical services; 
iv. that is flexible to adapt to the changing needs and has an effective grievance redressal 
mechanism and for matters connected therewith or incidental thereto. 
 
E.2 National Commission for Homoeopathy (NCH) 
The National Commission for Homoeopathy Act, 2020 received the assent of the 
President of India on the 20th of September 2020 and got published in the Gazette of India on 
21st September 2020. The Homoeopathy Central Council Act, 1973 (59 of 1973), has been 
repealed and replaced by the National Commission for Homoeopathy (NCH) Act, 2020 (15 of 
2020). The National Commission for Homoeopathy has been established under NCH Act, 
2020 and started functioning with effect from 05th July 2021 for following objectives. 
Objectives of the Commission: 
 
(i) Improve access to quality and affordable medical education in Homoeopathy. 
(ii) Ensure availability of adequate and high -quality Homoeopathy medical professionals 
in all parts of the country. 
(iii) Promote equitable and universal healthcare that encourages community health 
perspective and makes services of Homoeopathy medical professionals accessible and 
affordable to all the citizens. 
(iv) Promote national health goals. 
(v) Encourage Homoeopathy medical professionals to adopt the latest medical research in 
their work and to contribute to research. 
(vi) Periodic & transparent assessment of medical institutions. 
(vii) Facilitate maintenance of Homoeopathy Medical Register for India. 
(viii) Enforces high ethical standards in all aspects of medical services flexible to changing 
needs. 
(ix) Effective grievance redressal mechanism. 
 
F. National Medicinal Plants Board (NMPB) 
The resource base of Ayush medicines is largely plants. Increasing global interest in 
natural remedies has increased the demand for medicinal plants which are mainly sourced 
from the wild areas. This has led to the emergence of a number of issues like sustainability, 
conservation, cultivation, quality assurance, protection of Traditional Knowledge, issues

## Page 16

16 
 
related to access and benefit sharing etc. To coordinate all these matters relating to medicinal 
plants, Government of India has established the National Medicinal Plants Board (NMPB) 
under erstwhile Department of Ayush, Ministry of Health & Family Welfare. NMPB is the 
apex national body which coordinates all matters relating to medicinal plants in the country. 
The Board was established in November 2000 and acts as advisory body to the concerned 
Ministries, Departments and Agencies in strategic planning for medicinal plants related 
initiatives and to take measures to provide financial support to programmes relating to 
conservation, cultivation and the all-round development of the medicinal plants sector. 
 
G. STANDARDISATION OF ASU & H DRUGS 
Establishing the Pharmacopoeial standards for Ayurveda, Siddha, Unani and 
Homoeopathy medicine, both for single drugs and formulations, is essential, as 
Pharmacopoeial standards are important and are official for the implementation of the drug 
testing provisions under the Drugs and Cosmetics Act, 1940 and Rules thereunder. These 
standards are also essential to check samples of drugs available in the market for their desired 
quality in terms of identity, Purity & Strength. Government of India has been working on the 
task of developing Pharmacopoeial standards through Pharmacopoeia Commission for Indian 
Medicine & Homoeopathy (PCIM&H) by preparing official Formularies and 
Pharmacopoeias of Ayurveda, Siddha, Unani and Homoeopathy drugs so as to evolve 
uniform standards for preparation of drugs and to prescribe standards for single drugs as well 
as formulations used in the respective systems of medicine. 
Standards-setting exercise till date comprises of publication of Ayurvedic Formulary 
of India (Part I -IV) consisting of 1036 formulations, Siddha Formulary of India (Part I -II) 
consisting of 400 formulations and National Formulary of Unani Medicine (Part I -VI) 
consisting of 1230 formulations. Further pharmacopoeial monographs on 665 single drugs 
(Vol. I-X) and 203 formulations (Vol. I -IV) of Ayurveda, 139 single drugs (Vol. I -II) and 01 
formulation of Siddha, 298 single drugs (Vol. I -VI) and 201 formulations of Unani and 1117 
drugs (Vol. I-X) of Homoeopathy have been published. These pharmacopoeial standards and 
Formulary specifications also include stand -alone publications on Ayush Kvātha Cūrṇa / 
Kuṭinīr Cūraṇam / Safoof-i-Joshanda. 
 
(I) Pharmacopoeia Commission for Indian Medicine Homoeopathy (PCIM&H): 
The Pharmacopoeia Commission for Indian Medicine & Homoeopathy (PCIM&H) 
has been established as a Subordinate office of Ministry of Ayush, by winding up and 
merging into it three erstwhile institutes i.e. two subordinate offices namely Pharmacopoeia 
Laboratory for Indian Medicine (PLIM) and Homoeopathic Pharmacopoeia Laboratory (HPL) 
and an autonomous PCIM&H. with a common administrative control. 
Rule 163A to 163G of the Drugs Rules, 1945 assigns the role of establishing 
Pharmacopoeia standards and Formulary specifications for Indian systems of Medicine and 
Homoeopathy and to act as Central Drug Testing cum Appellate Laboratory for Indian 
systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy to PCIM&H. PCIM&H has key fields of activity 
related to Indian systems of Medicine & Homoeopathy viz.

## Page 17

17 
 
(i) Development and revision of Pharmacopoeial standards and Formulary specifications 
for ASU&H drugs. 
(ii) Acting as Central Drug Testing cum Appellate Laboratory for ASU&H drugs. 
(iii) Imparting training towards Capacity building of stakeholders in the field of Quality 
control of ASU&H drugs 
(iv) Establishing repositories for Raw Drug Reference Materials and Chemical Reference 
Materials for ASU&H drugs. 
(v) Development of Medicinal Plants Garden, Herbarium, Museum etc. 
 
PCIM&H has recently been accredited with the standard ISO/IEC 17025:2017 for 
competence of testing and calibration laboratory by National Accreditation Board for Testing 
and Calibration Laboratories (NABL) for analysis of biological and chemical parameters as 
per pharmacopoeial standards. PCIM&H is also marinating Integrated Management System 
(IMS) Certifications: IS/ISO 9001:2015 for Quality Management System (QMS), IS/ISO 
14001:2015 for Environmental Management System (EMS), and IS/ISO 45001:2018 for 
Occupational Health & Safety Management System (OHSMS) provided through Bureau of 
India Standards (BIS). The IMS certification system help us to assure the adherence to 
international acceptance of the pharmacopoeial standards developed by PCIM&H for 
ASU&H drugs. 
 
 (II) Indian Medicines Pharmaceutical Corporation Limited (IMPCL), Mohan 
(Almora): 
 
Indian Medicines Pharmaceutical Corporation Limited (IMPCL) is a Central Public 
Sector undertaking of the Government of India. It was incorporated in 1978 under the 
administrative control of Ministry of Ayush. The Government of India is presently holding 
98.11 per cent shares and 1.89 per cent shares is held by the Government of Uttarakhand 
through Kumaon Mandal Vikas Nigam Ltd. The Company is conferred as Mini -Ratna status 
based on the performance by Govt. of India. 
The Registered Office and Plant of the company is situated at Mohan (Via -
Ramnagar), Almora, Uttarakhand -244715 and Corporate Office at B -261, Okhla, Phase -I, 
NewDelhi-11020. The Company was set up with the objectives of Manufacturing and 
Marketing, genuine and efficacious Ayurvedic and Unani medicines, to the Central 
Government/State Govt. Hospitals and, Central Government Research units across the 
country. The company also has a reasonable presence to the open market/Trade Market. In 
addition to the manufacturing facilities the company has its own Ayush Drug Testing Lab for 
testing of Raw materials as well as finished products.

## Page 18

18 

H. REGULATION AND QUALITY CONTROL OF ASU& H DRUGS 
Ministry of Ayush has a Drug Policy Section (DPS) to undertake regulatory and 
quality control functions for Ayurveda, Siddha, Unani and Homoeopathy (ASU&H) drugs 
and implement drugs -related initiatives. The Drug Policy Section administers the 
provisions of the Drugs and Cosmetics Act, 1940 and Rules there under and Drugs and 
Magic Remedies (Objectionable Advertisements) Act, 1954 in respect of ASU&H drugs 
and associated matters. In this regard, the Section acts as a central drug control framework 
of ASU&H and coordinates with the State Licensing Authorities/Drug Controllers and drug 
manufacturers associations to achieve uniform administration of the legal provisions and 
for providing regulatory guidance, clarification and direction. 
Bringing Ayush into the mainstream health care delivery system of the country has 
long been a major policy objecti ve of the Ministry. Under the N HM, Ayush facilities are 
being set up in PHCs and CHCs and are being manned by qualified Ayush physicians 
appointed on contract basis. 
Since the creation of a separate Ministry, there has been a substantial increase in the 
infrastructural facilities under Ayush systems in the country.

SECTION - 2 
 
MEDICAL CARE FACILITIES 
 
The Indian System of Medicine & Homoeopathy treatments are popular with the masses and have 
proven strengths of treating common and chronic diseases. In order to make available the benefits of 
Ayush (Ayurveda, Unani, Siddha, Yoga & Naturopathy and Homoeopathy) to the public at large, it is very 
much important that adequate number of Ayush health care units (hospitals and dispensaries) be available 
in all pockets of the country, so that the public may exercise their choice in accessing the health services. 
 
This section provides an overall picture of Ayush hospitals and dispensaries existing in the country 
as well as in States/UTs along with their bed strength (for hospitals only) as on 01.04.2024. 
 
Hospitals and their Bed strength under Ayush systems: 
 
There has been a significant rise in the health care facilities under Ayush over the years. Number 
of Ayush hospitals have decreased* to 3648 as on 01.04.2024 from 3885 as on 01.04.2023. However, the 
Bed capacity of Ayush Hospitals as on 01.04.202 4 have increased to 63305 from 62670 as on 01.04.2023 
for all the systems. There is a preponderance of Ayurveda hospitals, as nearly 83% hospitals and 75% beds 
pertain to this system exclusively. Unani, Siddha, Naturopathy and Homoeopathy account for the 
remaining nearly 17% hospitals. 

 

 

 

 
Barring 58 hospitals existing under the control of 'CGHS & Central Government organizations', 
there are 3590 hospitals with 59966 beds in India as on 01.04.2024, which are under the jurisdiction of 
State Governments & Union Territories. Out of these, maximum number of hospitals are in the state of 
Uttar Pradesh (55.2%), followed by Uttarakhand (12.1%), Karnataka (7.6%) and Maharashtra (4.8%). 
 
*Tamil Nadu had earlier reported 289 Siddha hospitals; however, this number has now been revised to 26. 
The State has clarified that the earlier figure had inadvertently included hospitals without inpatient 
facilities (i.e., hospitals without beds).

## Page 49

1 
SECTION 3: 
 
MEDICAL MANPOWER 
 
Registration of practitioners under Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy, who possess 
recognized medical qualifications as per Indian Medicine Central Council Act, 1970 and Homoeopathy 
Central Council Act, 1973 is being done by State Boards/ Councils. These State Boards/Councils 
constituted by the State Governments are maintaining State Register of Ayush registered practitioners. 
Central Register of Ayush practitioners are also being prepar ed by the commissions, National 
Commission for Indian System of Medicine (NCISM) (erstwhile Central Council of Indian Medicine) and 
National Commission for Homoeopathy (NCH) (erstwhile Central Council of Homoeopathy ). Central 
Registers is being updated from time to time from State Registers. 
 
 This section provides an overall position of number of registered practitioners under each system 
of Ayush, IQ/NIQ, Gender wise and availability of Ayush practitioners/ doctors to the populace. 
 
Ayush doctors in India: 
 
As on 01.01.202 4, 
there are 7,84,962 
registered Ayush doctors/ 
practitioners (including 
1,14,380 Non-Institutionally 
Qualified) in India. Out of 
these 7,84,962 registered 
Ayush practitioners, 
3,71,957 (47.4%) belong to 
Ayurveda, while 3,44,443 
(43.9%) and 53,126 (6.8%) 
belong to, Homoeopathy & 
Unani Systems respectively. 
Only 10,269 (1.3%), 5,083 
(0.6%) and 84 (0.01%) 
doctors/ practitioners belong 
to Siddha, Naturopathy and 
Sowa-Rigpa Systems 
respectively. 
 
Growth of Ayush doctors since 1980: 
 
The n umber of registered Ayush doctors has shown an overall increasing trend from 1980 to 
2024. An average annual growth rate of 1. 7% was realized in registered Ayush practitioners during this 
period in 2024. 
 
* The average annual growth for naturopathy has been worked out for the period 1998-2024

## Page 50

2 

Significant variation in the availability of Ayush doctors per one crore population have been 
observed since 1980. On an aggregate, Ayush doctors serving per one crore population have varied from 
5582 to 5621 during the period 1980-2024 with a highest 6774 in 2000 and lowest at 4851 in 2019. 
 
Trends of Ayush doctors during the last eight years: 
 
The number of institutional qualified (IQ ) has increased from 5 ,57,890 to 6 ,70,582 from the year 
2017 to 202 4 and the number of Non -Institutional Qualified (NIQ) has decreased from 2,15,778 to 
1,14,380 in 2024.

State-wise availability of Ayush doctors: 
 
As on 01.01.202 4, the highest percentage of registration of Ayush doctors/practitioners has 
reported in the State of Maharashtra (24.3%) followed by Uttar Pradesh (1 2.5%) and Bihar (9.5%). About 
more than 50% of registration of total Ayush doctors/ practitioners has reported in the four major states 
Maharashtra, Uttar Pradesh, Bihar and Gujarat. 
 
State wise Ayush doctors per one crore population: 
As on 01.01.202 4, about 5,621 Ayush doctors per one crore population are available in India. 
Maharashtra and Kerala are the only state s where more than 1 4000 Ayush doctors i.e. (1 4,444 and 
15018) are serving per one crore population in 2024 respectively. States having 5 ,000 to 1 2,000 Ayush 
doctors serving per one crore population are West Bengal, Madhya Pradesh, Delhi, Punjab, Bihar, 
Telangana, Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh and Goa.

SECTION 4: MEDICAL EDUCATION 

Table 4.1 Ayush Colleges 
 
There are 971 Ayush colleges with admission capacity of 79593 conducting Under 
Graduate and Post Graduate courses in India as on 01.04.2024. 
 
Ayush Colleges imparting Under Graduate Courses 

 

There are 960 Ayush colleges with admission capacity of 71606 conducting 
Under Graduate courses in India as on 01.04.2024. About 56% of the colleges with 
56% admission capacity are of Ayurveda whereas about 28% of the colleges with 31% 
admission capacity belong to Homoeopathy. However, about 6 % of the c olleges with 
5% admission capacity pertain to Unani system. Remaining 10% of the colleges with 
8% admission capacity pertain to Siddha, Naturopathy and Sowa Rigpa systems of 
medicine.

Naturopathy education in India. Out of 71 Yoga and Naturopathy Colleges, and 15 are 
Government Colleges and 56 are Non-Government Colleges. 
 
Table 4.8 Homoeopathy Colleges 
 
As on 01.04.2024, there are 2 77 Homoeopathy Colleges with admission 
capacity of 24001 students and permitted seats of 20981 students imparting 
Homoeopathy education in India. Out of 2 77 Homoeopathy Colleges, and 4 1 are 
Government Colleges and 236 are Non-Government Colleges. 
 
Table 4.9 Sowa Rigpa Colleges 
 
As on 01.04.2024, there are 7 Sowa Rigpa Colleges with admission capacity of 
100 students and permitted seats of 85 students imparting S owa Rigpa education in 
India. Out of 7 Sowa Rigpa Colleges, and 3 are Government Colleges and 4 are Non-
Government. Sowa Rigpa Colleges exist in the 6 states namely Himachal Pradesh, 
Karnataka, Sikkim, Uttar Pradesh, West Bengal and UT namely Ladakh. 
 
Table 4.10 Exclusive Post Graduate Colleges 
 
As on 01.04.2024, there are 11 Exclusive Post Graduate Colleges / Institutions 
with admission capacity of 432 students and permitted s eats of 397 students. Four 
exclusive Unani post graduate college with total admission capacities and permitted 
seats of 150 and 147 respectively exists in the state of Jammu & Kashmir, Karnataka , 
Telangana and Uttar Pradesh. Whereas, three exclusive post graduate Ayurveda 
colleges with total admission capacity of 172 students and permitted seats of 172 
students are in the states of Delhi, Gujarat and West Bengal. 
 
 Four Exclusive Post Graduate Homoeopathy College each with total 
admission capacity of 110 students and permitted seats of 78 students are in the states 
of Delhi, Kerala, Maharashtra and Uttar Pradesh respectively. 
 
Annexure VII may be referred for State -wise list of Ayush Colleges in the 
Country as on 01.04.2024.

## Page 132

SECTION: 5 
 
LICENSED PHARMACIES UNDER AYUSH 

During the recent past, inclination of populace towards Ayush system of medicine has 
been observed. To meet the increased demand of Ayush medicines, and to provide Ayush 
medicines of reasonably good quality, there is a need to have licensed pharmacies for Ayush 
medicines with Good Manufacturing Practices (GMP) certificate. 
 
 As on 01.04.2024, there are 8604 Own Licensed Ayush Drug Manufacturing Units/ 
Licensed Pharmacies and 1921 Loan Licensed Ayush Drug Manufacturing Units in the 
country. Out of all Own L icensed Drug Manufacturing Units, GMP -compliant units 
comprised 94.29% of the total Drug M anufacturing Units, whereas; Non - GMP-compliant 
units comprised 5.71% of the total Drug Manufacturing Units. 

The following tables can be seen here under:

## Page 133

There are 29 states and U nion Territories of the country which have Ayush L icensed 
Pharmacies as on 01.04.2024. Uttar Pradesh has the Maximum number 1546 (18%) of Ayush 
Licensed Pharmacies. Maximum number of Ayurveda and Una ni pharmacies, viz., 1198 and 
318 respectively existed in the Uttar Pradesh, whereas, maximum number of Homoeopathy 
Licensed P harmacies, viz., 130 existed in West Bengal and Siddha Licensed P harmacies 
existed in the State of Tamil Nadu (285).

SECTION 6: INTEGRATION OF AYUSH IN THE HEALTH CARE 
SYSTEM UNDER NATIONAL HEALTH MISSION (NHM) 
 
 After the launch of National Health Mission (NHM), an initiative has been taken by the 
Department of Health & Family Welfare and Ministry of Ayush for strengthening of health care 
facilities at all levels by integrating Ayush systems in national healt h care delivery systems 
under NHM. 
 
Physical and functional in tegration of Ayush systems in N HM is most important 
strategy of m ainstreaming under N HM. Integration is facilitated by appointing or co -locating 
Ayush doctors & supporting staff and creating infrastructure according to local needs. Ayush 
doctors who are being appointed are used to facilitate management of programmes, 
monitoring and effective implementation of various interventions under NHM. 
 
There are 764 District Hospitals (DHs), 6090 Community Health Centres (CHCs) and 
26526 Primary Health Centres (PHCs) in rural areas in 767 districts, 3606 blocks and 707863 
villages in India as on March, 2024. 
 
Table 6.1 
 
Co-location Rate of Rural Health Infrastructure with Ayush facilities in India:

co-located with 469 District

Centres and 6628 Primary Health

also been co -located with 195 
‘Other than CHC or above block

health facilities above Sub Centres 
(SC) but below block level’.

facilities available in

as on 31.03.2024 . Moreover, 6549 Ayush Pharmacist/Paramedical staff has been appointed 
as on 31.03.2024 . Under RBSK Programme, Maharashtra has appointed the maxim um 
number of Ayush Doctors (2 275). Maximum number of paramedical staff has been appointed 
in the State of Gujarat (1646).

SECTION-7: OUTLAY AND EXPENDITURE 

This section records total Union Budget allocation for the Ministry of Ayush d uring the 
financial year 2023-24 . It contains budgetary allocations for Establishment Expenditure of the 
Centre, Statutory Institutions, Autonomous Bodies under the Ministry and for various Central 
Sector & Centrally Sponsored Schemes. 
 
An outlay of Rs. 3647.50 crore (BE) has been earmarked for the Ministry of Ayush in 
the current financial year and it is revised to Rs 3000.00 crore at the Revised Estimate (RE) 
Stage. Ministry of Ayush has utilized an amount of Rs. 2916.31crore (97.2% of RE ) during 
the financial year 2023-24. 
 
For strengthening of Ayush Delivery Sys tems, the Centre granted Rs. 135.00 crore at 
the RE Stage and an expenditure of Rs. 129.59 crore incurred during the 2023 -24 fiscal year. 
Strengthening of Ayush Delivery Systems include allocation to various Central Sector 
Schemes of Ministry of Ayush. 
 
The funds earmarked at the RE Stage for Statutory Institutions and Autonomous Bodies 
are Rs. 194.69 crore and 1763.93 crore respectively. For the same, Ministry of Ayush has 
utilized an amount of Rs. 185.37 crore and Rs. 1651.11 crore during the financial year 2023 -
24. 
 
 To provide cost effective and equitable Ayush health care throughout the country, 
Ministry of Ayush has approved a fund of Rs. 15041.234 lakhs in the fiscal year 2023 -24 for 
setting up of the Integrated Ayush Hospitals under National Ayush Mission (NAM). 
 
 To revitalize and strengthen the Ayush systems making them as prominent medical 
streams in addressing the health care of the society through upgrading the standalone Ayush 
Hospitals and Dispensaries, Ministry of Ayush has approved the amount of Rs. 4454.24 lakhs 
& Rs. 1330.91 lakhs under recurring units & non - recurring units of standalone Ayush 
Hospitals respectively and also an amount of Rs. 995.65 lakhs & Rs 4854.86 lakhs under 
recurring units & non - recurring units of standalone Ayush Dispensaries respectively under 
National Ayush Mission (NAM). 
 
 To strengthen institutional capacity at the state level through Setting up/ upgradation of 
Ayush Educational Institutions, Ministry of Ayush has approved an amount of Rs. 3020.52 
lakhs including Sta te share in the fiscal year 2023 -24 for setting up of Ayush Educational 
Institutions under National Ayush Mission (NAM) and an amount of Rs.3302.05 lakhs 
including Sta te share in the fiscal year 2023 -24 for upgradation of Ayush Educational 
Institutions under NAM.

## Page 174

SECTION 8 AYUSH RELATED FOREIGN TRADE 
 The export and import of Ayush related products takes place under the Indian T rade 
Classification based on Harmonized System of Coding (ITCHS) through 74 HS codes . However, 
it may be noted that most of the Products of Ayurveda, Homoeopathic, Siddha, Sowa Rigpa and 
Unani systems, Herbal Products and Medicinal Plant products are not identified under specific 
HS Codes (International Harmonised Commodity Description and Coding System), hence the 
actual quantum of exports and imports is expected to be at a higher side than figures reported 
under 74 HS Codes. 
The India’s total export value of Ayush related products increased from Rs.5392 crores in FY 19 
to Rs.10790 crores in FY23 (100% increase). 
The India’s total import value of Ayush related products increased from Rs.2269 crores in FY 19 
to Rs.3803 crores in FY23 (67% increase).

G1: Line Graph on Ayush exports over the last five years 
G2: Line Graph on Ayush imports over the last five years

## Page 189

SECTION- 9 
NEW INITIATIVES 
The Ministry of Ayush is determ ined to revive and promote the Traditional Indian Medicine 
System. In a bid to reach out to the masses, industries and the Ayush fraternity, Ministry has taken 
various initiatives and steps including digitalization of the Ayush Sector. 2023-24 marked a 
transformative year for the Ministry of Ayush, showcasing its global influence, commitment to 
traditional medicine, and efforts to enhance healthcare on a national and international scale . Central 
Regulatory Bodies, Central Research Councils, National Institutes, All India Institutes, National 
Medicinal Plants Board, Pharmacopoeia Commission of Indian Medicine & Homeopathy and 
Pharmacopoeia Laboratories of Ayush established by the Ministry of Ayush and the network of Ayush 
institutions in the states is being expanded and strengthened and are taking various new initiatives for 
promoting and propagating Indian Systems of Medicine and Homoeopathy at the national and 
international level. Further, financial support is provided through Centrally Sponsored and Central 
Sector Schemes for various activities in the field of Ayush including infrastructure development, 
research, capacity building, supply of medicines to the health facilities etc. These are some of 
Ministry’s initiatives and achievements during 2023-24 among many. 
I. Education 
i. MoU between Institute of Teaching & Research in Ayurveda (ITRA), Jamnagar, Ministry of 
Ayush and with National Institute of Health, the Republic of Peru on 15 th April 2023 for 
academic collaboration in the field Avurveda. 
ii. SMART Program: The National Commission for Indian System of Medicine (NCISM) and the 
Central Council for Research in Ayurvedic Sciences (CCRAS), for regulating medical education 
and conducting scientific research respectively, have launched ‘SMART’ (Scope for 
Mainstreaming Ayurveda Research in Teaching Professionals) program aimed to boost scientific 
research in priority healthcare research areas through Ayurveda colleges and hospitals. 
 
iii. Separate Academic Department of Ayush in AIIMS : Decided in the 7 th meeting of Central 
Institute Body (CIB) of AIIMS held in 2023 under the Chairpersonship of the Hon'ble Cabinet 
Minister of Health and Family Welfare. This initiative aims to integrate education in modern and 
traditional medicine, fostering collaboration and synergy between the two disciplines. 
iv. Inauguration of the Central Research Institute of Yoga & Naturopathy: Prime Minister Shri 
Narendra Modi inaugurated the Central Research Institute of Yoga & Naturopathy (CRIYN) 
with a 200-bed hospital facility at Devarkhana Village, Jhajjar. The establishment of the central 
institute reflects the government’s commitment to advancing research and providing state-of-the-
art Yoga and Naturopathy healthcare facilities. The Hon’ble PM also inaugurated ‘Nisarg Gram’ 
under the National Institute of Naturopathy, Pune, in the virtual presence of Shri Eknath Shinde, 
Chief Minister, Maharashtra. 
 
v. Chintan Shivir 
The inaugural "Chintan Shivir" provided a roadmap for Ayush's future, focusing on digital 
health, strategy, challenges, and collaboration, reinforcing its commitment to growth. 
 
vi. Centre of Excellence

## Page 190

Ministry has undertaken various Collaborative researches following an integrative research 
model for scientific evidence generation of Ayush medicines at Institutions of national repute 
like CSIR, CIMR, ILBS etc. 
AyurTech IIT Jodhpur: Ministry of Ayush under Centre of Excellence (CoE) scheme has 
sponsored the AyurTech centre at the IIT Jodhpur. The CoE AyurTech is part of the Centre of 
Excellence in Artificial Intelligence (AI) based Precision Healthcare at the school of Artificial 
Intelligence and Data Science (AIDE) at IIT Jodhpur. The aim of this centre is to establish an AI -
driven integrative framework for population and individual risk stratification and early actionable 
precision health interventions. The Centre for AyurTech is being proposed as the first -of-its-kind 
initiative in the precision health and medicine space that would combine Electronics, Digital health 
and AI and multi -omics technologies for realising ‘Evidence -based Ayurveda’ solutions in a 
transdisciplinary framework to establish AI -driven integrative framework for population and 
individual risk stratification and early health interventions. 
vii. Increased awareness and interest in formal Ayush education in different countries 
Ministry of Ayush has collaborated with more than 50 countries for the promotion of Ayurveda, 
Yoga and all other Ayush systems by virtue of signing of country -to-country MoU, research 
collaborations, MoU for establishing academic chairs of Ayush in foreign universities, setting up 
of Ayurveda/Ayush hospitals/academic institute, setting up of herbal garden, exchange of 
experts, deputation of Ayush experts, organizing workshops, conferences etc. Moreover, every 
year 104 seats are allocated to foreign countries coming from different parts of the world to learn 
Ayurveda, Yoga, Unani etc. respective Ayush systems. The students are provided scholarships, 
tuition fees, to and fro air fare etc. to study Ayush systems in India. 
 
II. Technology 
i. Ayush has been on boarded in Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission since its inception. 
ii. MoA has undertaken 20+ key Digital initiatives under (1) Health information system (2) 
Research database/library (3) Academic (4) Information Education and Communication (IEC) 
categories. 
iii. Initiatives under Health information system: 
a. The Ayush hospital management information system (A-HMIS), 
b. National Ayush Morbidity and Standardized Terminologies Electronic Portal 
(NAMASTE), 
c. Ayush Suraksha, 
d. e-Aushadhi, 
e. e-Charak, 
f. Triskandha Kosha, 
g. Siddha initiative for documentation of drug Adverse Reactions (SiddAR) App. 
iv. Initiatives under Research database/library: 
a. The Traditional Knowledge Digital Library (TKDL), 
b. Ayush research portal, 
c. DHARA, 
d. e-CHLAS (Current Health Literature Awareness Services), 
e. Research Management Information system (RMIS), 
f. e-Grantha samuccaya 
g. Ayush Sanjivani App 
v. Academic initiatives: 
a. The Ayurveda e-learning and Ayurvedic Inheritance of India

## Page 191

vi. Information, education and communication (IEC) initiatives: 
a. The Siddha–NIS App, 
b. Yoga locator, and 
c. Naturopathy-NIN App 
vii. Ministry of Ayush has developed an Ayush research portal which is having huge repository of 
extensive research works by various stakeholders in Ayush. The portal is actively managed by 
CCRAS, Ministry of Ayush and is made in a searchable format and includes publications in indexed 
journals. This provide a showcase of evidence based Ayush systems and a ready solution of 
researchers and academicians. 
viii. Onboarding of Ministry of Ayush to the PM GatiShakti Portal: PM GatiShakti National Master 
Plan (PMGS -NMP) was launched on 13 th October 2021 for providing multimodal connectivity 
infrastructure to various economic zones. Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA) 
accorded approval for the implementation of PM GatiShakti National Master Plan on 21 st October 
2021. In the year 2023, as part of onboarding exercise of social sector ministry onto the portal, the 
Ministry of Ayush has been on boarded onto to PM GatiShakti Portal. PM GatiShakti portal is a 
digital system where individual Ministries will be given separate user identification (login ids) to 
update their data on a periodic basis. The data of all the individual Ministries will be integrated in 
one platform which will be available for planning, review and monitoring. The Logistics Division, 
DPIIT, Ministry of Commerce & Industry will further assist all the stakeholders through BISAG -N 
for creating and updating their required layers in the system and update their database through 
Application Programming Interface (APIs). To facilitate the capturing accurate geographical 
locations of Ayush Assets BISAG -N with the coordination of DPIIT has developed a “PM 
GatiShakti-Ayush Asset Mapping Tool (PGAAMT)” for the Ministry. The tool is being used to 
capture the details of the following six layers viz. 1. Ayush Health Facilities (AH), 2. Ayush 
Education Facilities (AE), 3. Ayush Research (AR), 4. Medicinal Plant Facilities (MP), 5. Ayush 
Drug and Food Materials Facility (ADFM) and 6. Ayush Heritage facilities (AHM). Further, the 
development of GIS based decision making tools for various Ayush initiatives viz., funding for 
Medicinal Plant Conservation, Collection etc., (through NMPB), granting permission to the Ayush 
educational institutes (Through NCISM, NCH) etc., is in progress. 
ix. PCIM&H has developed the QR code enabled database of medicinal plants used in the Ayurveda, 
Siddha, Unani and Homoeopathic system of medicines. In the first stage, PCIM&H has developed 
the database for 10 medicinal plants. 
 
III. Integrated Health 
➢ The Prime Minister's mention of 'Ayush' in "Mann Ki Baat" significantly contributed to the 
sector's growth and it was compiled and published in a special issue of JRAS Journal of CCRAS. 
Ministry initiated many collaborative and integrative researches with Basic Science 
Ministries/departments and reputed scientific organizations at national and international levels. 
➢ A Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) was signed between the Ministry of Ayush and the 
Indian Council of Medical Research (ICMR) to promote and collaborate on Integrative Health 
Research. This cooperation will explore the possibility to work on public health research, take 
initiatives for addressing diseases of national importance and look at possibilities of conducting 
high-quality clinical trials jointly on identified areas/disease conditions of national importance 
with promising therapies of Ayush system t o generate evidence for wider acceptance. The MoU 
will facilitate looking at the possibility of inclusion of research on Integrative Medicine in the 
“National ethical guidelines for biomedical and health research involving human participants” by

## Page 192

ICMR-DHR. This MoU will also strengthen research capacity through training of Ayush 
researchers. 
➢ Launch of Joint Initiative of Ministry of Ayush and Ministry of Tribal Affairs to address the 
health needs of children in Eklavya Model Residential Schools (ERMS). 
➢ Shri Sarbananda Sonowal, Hon'ble Union Minister of Ayush and Ports Shipping and Waterways 
and Dr. Mansukh Mandaviya, Hon'ble Union Minister of Health & Family Welfare jointly 
inaugurated the Department of Integrative Medicine at Safdarjung hospital, New Delhi. The 
Department of Integrative Medicine will offer services in specialty OPD, Panchakarma Therapy, 
and diet consultation for the patients. 
➢ The Central Council for Research in Ayurvedic Sciences and Lady Hardinge Medical College 
and Hospital signed a MoU for establishing and integrated medicine centre. The integrated 
centre will help for collecting research data for the impact of integrated treatment approach of 
allopathy as well as Ayurveda in disease management. 
➢ Ministry of Ayush and the Department of Ex -servicemen Contributory Health Scheme (ECHS) 
has signed a MoU to integrate Ayurveda in 10 ECHS polyclinics across India. This MoU will 
further expand Ayush health services in defence sector. In addition, there are already operating 
Ayurveda Centres in 12 Military Hospitals of AFMC and 37 Cantonment Board Hospitals. 
➢ Establishment of Ayush Holistic Wellness Center and MoU with the Hon’ble Supreme Court of 
India: The then Hon’ble Chief Justice of India, Justice DY Chandrachud, inaugurated ‘Ayush 
Holistic Wellness Centre’ in the premises of the Supreme Court of India in the august presence 
of Companion Judges of the Supreme Court of India; Union Cabinet Minister of Ayush and 
Ports, Shipping & Waterways Shri Sarbananda Sonowal and Minister of State for Ayush Dr. 
Munjpara Mahendrabhai. On this occasion, the Supreme Court and the All -India Institute of 
Ayurveda signed a MoU regarding establishing, operationalising and providing expert services 
at the Ayush Holistic Wellness Centre. 
➢ MoU with WCD Ministry for nutritional improvement in adolescent girls: The Ministry of 
Ayush and the Ministry of Women and Child Development have signed a MoU for the 
nutritional improvement in adolescent girls through Ayurveda Interventions. It is a Joint Public 
Health Initiative for “Anaemia Control among adolescent girls using Ayurveda interventions in 
the five districts under Mission Utkarsh”. The MoU was signed in the presence of Union 
Minister of Ayush Shri Sarbananda Sonowal and Union Minister of Women and Child 
Development Smt. Smriti Irani. 
➢ Mega joint initiatives between the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare and the Ministry of 
Ayush: Dr Mansukh Mandaviya, Union Minister of Health and Family Welfare, launched six 
Ayush-ICMR Advanced Centres for Integrated Health Research in four AIIMS (Delhi, Jodhpur, 
Nagpur, Rishikesh). 
➢ A joint initiative taken by Central Council for Research in Ayurvedic Sciences (CCRAS) in 
collaboration with the Indian Council of Medical Research for conducting a community based 
pioneering research study on anemia control using Ayurveda Medicines: This Study will take 
place through 8 Study centres at MGIMS Wardha, AIIMS Jodhpur, NITM Bengaluru, RIMS 
Ranchi, KEM Hospital Research Center and AIIMS Bibinagar. 
➢ Ministry of Ayush l aunched the Indian Public Health (IPH) Standards for Ayush healthcare 
facilities: These IPH Standards mark a signif icant step towards ensuring uniform quality in 
Ayush infrastructure, human resources, and medicines and to further enhance Ayush healthcare 
services nationwide by establishing consistent benchmarks for facilities across the country.

➢ International Diplomacy and Collaborations: In 2024, the Ministry of Ayush continued to

of traditional medicine. Additionally, India signed a Memorandum of Understanding (MoU)
